[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-06
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Event Space#104 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"The 12-hour gap between 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00' and 'Plaza#206 at 20:00' is implausibly long for typical daily activities without further context."
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-07
- Trajectory: ['Buddhist Temple#854 at 00:10', 'Noodle House#690 at 00:50', 'Shrine#1350 at 08:00', 'Farmers Market#81 at 10:00', 'Art Museum#291 at 13:00', 'Sake Bar#1823 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-09
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 07:40', 'Discount Store#10 at 12:00', 'Plaza#206 at 18:00', 'Event Space#104 at 20:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"Discount Store#10 at 12:00 is a plausible time for shopping, but the preceding 4.5-hour gap between leaving the hotel and arriving at the store, and the subsequent 6-hour gap before reaching the plaza, are unusually long for typical urban activities."

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-17
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#2410 at 12:00', 'Plaza#206 at 19:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-18
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#2410 at 12:00', 'Plaza#206 at 18:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 20:00', 'Karaoke Bar#174 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`

Noodle House#690 at 20:00 for dinner and Karaoke Bar#174 at 22:00 for evening entertainment are all temporally logical and follow a common urban daily rhythm."

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-19
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Park#2410 at 08:30', 'Plaza#206 at 19:00', 'Discount Store#300 at 21:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "The time elapsed between visiting the Park and the Plaza (08:30 to 19:00) is 10.5 hours, which is an unusually long gap for typical daily activities without explicit intermediate events."
}
```

- Date: 2019-12-19
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Park#2410 at 08:30', 'Plaza#206 at 19:00', 'Discount Store#300 at 20:30', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]

    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-20
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Plaza#206 at 19:00', 'Event Space#104 at 21:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-24
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#2410 at 18:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 22:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-12-25
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Event Space#104 at 22:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:15']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Plaza#206 at 20:00 followed by Event Space#104 at 22:00 on Christmas Day is plausible for evening festivities, but the long gap between Hotel#1377 at 08:00 and Plaza#206 at 20:00 implies a significant unstated activity or prolonged inactivity that requires further context."
}
```
